\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}+\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}+\left(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\right)(\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})]-\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})])\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\Bigg[\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}+\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}+\left|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\right|\Bigg]\coloneqq\mathcal{E}^{*}

(25)

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\left|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\right|-\!\left(\!\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\!\right)\!(\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})]\!-\!\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})]\!)\Bigg].

Applying Lemma
B.1
to the above equation,
we conclude that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg]

(26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg].

Now we derive the necessary and sufficient condition for
the optimal scoring function of
\mathcal{E}
.

Necessary condition.

If
s
is an optimal scoring function such that
\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)=0
, the by Eq. (
26
), there holds

\displaystyle 0\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)\leq 0.

Therefore,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg]=0,

implying that
for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
with
{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}\neq{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]=0,

\displaystyle\Longrightarrow

\displaystyle\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))>0.

Sufficient condition.

Let a scoring function
s
satisfies that, for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
with
{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}\neq{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}
, there holds

\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))>0.

Then for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
, there holds

\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|=0.

Then according to Eq. (
26
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\!\Bigg[\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg]=0,

indicating that
\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)=0.

This shows that
s
is an optimal scoring function.

Hence,
s
is an optimal scoring function
if and only if
for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
with
{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}\neq{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}/{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}
, there holds

\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))>0.

The proof is completed.
∎

B.3
Proof of Theorem
3.4

Before we provide the proof of Theorem
3.4
, we introduce Pinsker’s inequality which relates KL divergence to total variation.

Lemma B.2

(Pinsker’s Inequality)
.

Let
\mu
and
\nu
be two probability measures on a measurable space
(\Omega,\mathcal{F})
, with
\nu\ll\mu
(i.e.,
\nu
is absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu
) so that the Radon-Nikodym derivative
\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}
exists.
The KL divergence of
\nu
with respect to
\mu
is defined as:

D_{\text{KL}}(\nu\parallel\mu)=\int_{\Omega}\log\left(\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}\right)d\nu,

where
D_{\text{KL}}(\nu\parallel\mu)=+\infty
if the integral is infinite. The total variation distance between
\mu
and
\nu
is:

\|\mu-\nu\|_{\text{TV}}=\sup_{A\in\mathcal{F}}|\mu(A)-\nu(A)|=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\Omega}\left|\frac{d\nu}{d\mu}-1\right|d\mu.

Pinsker’s inequality relates these two quantities by the bound:

\|\mu-\nu\|_{\text{TV}}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}D_{\text{KL}}(\nu\parallel\mu).

Proof of Theorem
3.4
.

According to Lemma
3.2
,
we choose an optimal scoring function

s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\tau\log\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}.

(27)

Then
\mathcal{L}^{*}=\mathcal{L}(s_{*})=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\log\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}
.
For a scoring function
s
, we define

\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau).

Then
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp(\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)=1
. Let
g(\mathbf{x})=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)
. We have

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\tilde{s})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})>\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})]=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-g(\mathbf{x})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g(\mathbf{x})]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})>s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})]=\mathcal{E}({s}).

There also holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s})

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp((\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\tilde{s}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))/\tau)

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp((s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g(\mathbf{x})+g(\mathbf{x})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))/\tau)

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp((s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))/\tau)=\mathcal{L}(s).

Therefore,
\mathcal{E}(\tilde{s})=\mathcal{E}({s}),\mathcal{L}(\tilde{s})=\mathcal{L}(s)
we can replace
{s}
by
\tilde{s}
. In other words, without loss of generality, we can make the following regularity assumption

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)=1.

(28)

Let the distribution
q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}
be defined in Eq. (
22
), i.e.,

q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})=\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}=p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau).

By Eq. (
23
), we have

\mathcal{L}(s)-\mathcal{L}(s_{*})=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}||q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}).

According to Pinsker’s inequality (Lemma
B.2
), we can bound the KL-divergence by total variation distance:

D_{\mathrm{KL}}(p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}||q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+})\geq\frac{1}{2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{Y}}|p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})-q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})|d\mathbf{y}\Bigg)^{2}.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(s)-\mathcal{L}(s_{*})

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{Y}}|p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})-q_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})|d\mathbf{y}\Bigg)^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{Y}}|p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})-p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|d\mathbf{y}\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{Y}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)\Bigg|d\mathbf{y}\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\int_{\mathcal{Y}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\Bigg|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)\Bigg|d\mathbf{y}\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\Bigg|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)\Bigg|\Bigg)^{2},

(29)

where the third equality is due to Eq. (
27
).

Then we make the following decomposition that introduces the pair
\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}[|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|+|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|].

(30)

We will show that for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|+|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|.

(31)

It is trivial when RHS is equal to
0
. We consider the case

\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0,\quad\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\neq 0.

Without loss of generality, suppose that

\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}>\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})},\quad s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq 0.

By the definition of
s_{*}
in Eq. (
27
), we have

\displaystyle\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)-\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)=

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}>0.

(32)

and
\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)\geq 0
.
Therefore,

\displaystyle|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|+|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)-\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))+\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|(\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)-\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))+(\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)-\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau))+(\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)-\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)=\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}.

As a result, Eq. (
B.3
) holds true.
Plugging Eq. (
B.3
) into Eq. (
30
) yields

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|.

(33)

Combining Eqs. (
B.3
), (
B.3
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}(s)-\mathcal{L}(s_{*})\geq\frac{\tau}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\Bigg(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|\exp(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau))-\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)|\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2}\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0]\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg)^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\tau}{2}(\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s))^{2},

where the second inequality is due to Jensen’s inequality
and the last inequality results from Eq. (
26
).
As a result,

\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)\leq\sqrt{2/\tau}\sqrt{\mathcal{L}(s)-\mathcal{L}(s_{*})}.

The proof is completed by noting that
s_{*}
is a minimizer of
\mathcal{L}(s)
and thus
\mathcal{L}(s_{*})=\mathcal{L}^{*}
.
∎

Appendix C
Proofs in Section
4.1

Recall the error decomposition

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\!-\!\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Big|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|}_{\text{inner error}}+\underbrace{\Big|\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|}_{\text{outer error}}.

We will control inner error and outer error separately. Before that, we provide the proof of Lemma
4.2
.

C.1
Proof of Lemma
4.2

Proof of Lemma
4.2
.

For
\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}
, we define

d_{i}(\mu_{i})=\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)+\mu_{i}-\tau.

Then we have

d^{\prime}_{i}(\mu_{i})=\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)\Bigg(\frac{-1}{\tau}\Bigg)\!\!+1=1-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)\!\!.

By taking
d_{i}^{\prime}(\mu_{i})=0
, we obtain the minimizer of
d_{i}
:

\mu_{i}^{*}=\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})}{\tau}\!\right).

Plugging it into
d_{i}
, we have

\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\!\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)\!\!+\mu_{i}-\tau=d_{i}(\mu_{i}^{*})=\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})}{\tau}\!\right).

Since
s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-B,B]
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{Y},\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}
,
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})=s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\in[-2B,2B]
, implying that
\mu_{i}^{*}\in[-2B,2B]
. Therefore,

\min_{\mu_{i}\in[-2B,2B]}\!\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)\!\!+\!\!\mu_{i}-\tau=\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})}{\tau}\!\right).

Taking summation over all
i\in[n]
, we get

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})}{\tau}\!\right)

\displaystyle=-\tau+\!\!\frac{1}{n}\!\sum_{i=1}^{n}\!\Bigg[\!\min_{|\mu_{i}|\leq 2B}\!\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\!\frac{\Delta(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\!\right)\!\!+\!\!\mu_{i}\!\Bigg].

This completes the proof of Eq. (
13
).

To prove Eq. (
14
), we define

d(\mu)=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)\!+\!\mu-\tau,\qquad\mu\in\mathbb{R}.

Then

d^{\prime}(\mu)=-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)+1.

Taking
d^{\prime}(\mu)=0
obtains the optimal value
\mu^{*}=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)
.
Therefore,

\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\!\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)\!+\!\mu=d(\mu^{*})=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)-\tau.

By noting that
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
, there holds

\min_{|\mu|\in[-2B,2B]}\!\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)\!+\!\mu=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)-\tau.

Taking expectation w.r.t.
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
and combining with Eq. (
5
) complete the proof of the lemma.
∎

C.2
Proof of Lemma
4.3

Proof of Lemma
4.3
.

By Eqs. (
13
), (
14
), there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)-\tau+\mu\Bigg]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{\prime}\}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\min_{\mu_{i}\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\tau+\mu_{i}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg\{\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)-\tau+\mu\Bigg]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)-\tau+\mu\Bigg]\Bigg\}.

Let
\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=\mu+\tau[\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu)/\tau)-1]
. Since
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\in[-2B,2B]
,
\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
is
\exp(-4B/\tau)/\tau
-strongly convex and
\exp(4B/\tau)
-Lipschitz w.r.t.
\mu
over the interval
[-2B,2B]
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\}}\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg].

For simplicity, we define
S^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}
, and

\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})=\argmin_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}).

Then for any
S^{\prime}
,

\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}(\{S^{\prime}\});\mathbf{y}^{\prime}).

Taking expectation for
S^{\prime}
yields

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime}).

(34)

By the
\exp(-4B/\tau)/\tau
-strong convexity of
\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
,
we can define a convex function

\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\coloneq\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{\exp(-4B/\tau)}{2\tau}\mu^{2}.

Then

\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})=\argmin_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})=\argmin_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\bigg\{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})+\frac{\exp(-4B/\tau)}{2\tau}\mu^{2}\bigg\},

which is the regularized empirical risk minimization algorithm.
According to Theorem 12, 22 in
12
, for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{w}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]\leq\frac{2\exp(4B/\tau)\tau}{\exp(-4B/\tau)m}=\frac{2\exp(8B/\tau)\tau}{m}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})+\frac{\exp(-4B/\tau)}{2\tau}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime}))^{2}

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})-\frac{\exp(-4B/\tau)}{2\tau}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime}))^{2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]\leq\frac{2\exp(8B/\tau)\tau}{m}.

Combined with Eq. (
34
), for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{w}
, there holds

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}}\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]\leq\frac{2\exp(8B/\tau)\tau}{m}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big[\mathcal{L}(\mathbf{w})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})\Big]=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}}\min_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{2\exp(8B/\tau)\tau}{m}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Remark C.1
.

The term
\exp(8B/\tau)

arises from standard Lipschitz and strong convexity arguments applied to the exponential function over bounded domains, and is often standard in the generalization theory
(
25
)
. The exponential dependences also appear in previous theoretical analyses of CRL
(
51
;
35
)
.
In practical implementations such as CLIP and SimCLR, the scoring function typically uses cosine similarity, which naturally bounds
B\in[-1,1]
.
The primary focus of our work is to characterize how
m

and
n

influence the scaling behavior of generalization, rather than obtaining tight numerical constants. We believe it would be an interesting future problem to achieve tighter bounds and remove these exponential dependencies.

C.3
Proof of Lemma
4.4

Proof of Lemma
4.4
.

For
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}
, we introduce
k_{\mathbf{w}}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}^{m+1}\to\mathbb{R}
as follows:

k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})=\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})}{\tau}\Bigg).

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}\Big[k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im}).

By Proposition
A.1
, it suffices to bound Rademacher complexity
\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{K})
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{K}

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})\mapsto k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}):\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\right\},

\displaystyle S

\displaystyle=\{(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{11},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1m}),\cdots,(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{n1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{nm})\}.

Recall that

\displaystyle S_{1}\coloneq\{(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{ij}^{-}):1\leq i\leq n,1\leq j\leq m\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}\coloneq\{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\mapsto\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}):\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\}.

Let
k_{\mathbf{w}},k_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}
be two functions in
\mathcal{K}
. By Lemma
E.4
, the logistic loss
\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp(t_{j}/\tau)
is
1
-
\ell_{\infty}
-Lipschitz, i.e.

\Bigg|\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp(t_{j}/\tau)-\tau\log\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp(t_{j}^{\prime}/\tau)\Bigg|\leq\max_{j}|t_{j}-t_{j}^{\prime}|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\|k_{\mathbf{w}}-k_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{2}(S)}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})-k_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i}|k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})-k_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i}\max_{j}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})\in S_{1}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})|=\|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{\infty}(S_{1}))}.

As a result, for any
\mathbf{\epsilon}>0
,

\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{K},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))\leq\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{\infty}(S_{1})).

From Assumption
4.1
,
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\in[-2B,2B]
for all
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
, then
k_{\mathbf{w}}\in[-2B,2B]
, implying that

B_{\mathcal{K}}\coloneq\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\|k_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{2}(S)}\leq 2B.

According to Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
A.5
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{K})\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha>0}4\alpha+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha}^{B_{\mathcal{K}}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{K},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{K},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{\infty}(S_{1}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2B}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg)}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}(2B-{1}/{n})\lesssim\frac{B\sqrt{\log(1+{8nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}}{n}.

Using Proposition
A.1
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big[\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}\Big[k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}k_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im}).

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{K})+12B\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}\lesssim\frac{B\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+B\sqrt{\log(1+{8nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}}{\sqrt{n}}.

The inequality also holds for the other side following a similar manner. The proof is completed.
∎

Appendix D
Proof of Theorem
4.6

Recall that

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})|\leq\underbrace{\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|}_{\text{outer error}}+\underbrace{\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\bigl)\Bigg|}_{\text{inner error}},

where

h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg),\quad\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\tau\log\Bigg(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg).

We will control each component respectively.

D.1
Estimation of the outer error

We define the following function class and data space:

\mathcal{H}_{1}=\{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mapsto h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\},\quad S_{4}=\{(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{1}),\cdots,(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n})\}.

To bound the outer error,
we control the covering number
\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))
.

Lemma D.1
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, then for any
\mathbf{\epsilon}>0
, we have

\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg).

Proof.

Let
h_{\mathbf{w}},h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}
be two functions in
\mathcal{H}_{1}
. For
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{Y}
, we aim bound
|h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|
. Without loss of generality, we assume
h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\geq h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
. Then

\displaystyle|h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|

\displaystyle=h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg)-\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\tau\log\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\tau\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\tau\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)}{\exp(-2B/\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\tau\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}|\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)-\exp(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau)|}{\exp(-2B/\tau)}

\displaystyle\leq\tau\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp(2B/\tau)|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/\tau|}{\exp(-2B/\tau)}

\displaystyle=\exp(4B/\tau)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|,

where the first inequality is due to
\log(t)\leq t-1
, the second inequality results from
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=s_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
, the last inequality is by the
\exp(2B/\tau)
-Lipschitzness of
\exp(t)
over the interval
[-2B/\tau,2B/\tau]
.

We choose a cover
\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}
of
\mathcal{W}
such that Eq. (
17
) holds. By Eq. (
21
), we know that

\displaystyle|h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq

\displaystyle\exp(4B/\tau)\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\exp(4B/\tau)\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in\{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\exp(4B/\tau)\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}\leq 4\exp(4B/\tau)\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon.

As a result,

\|h_{\mathbf{w}}-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{2}(S_{4})}\leq 4\exp(4B/\tau)\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon

For
\mathbf{\epsilon}>0
, choosing

\varepsilon=\frac{\mathbf{\epsilon}}{4\exp(4B/\tau)\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}}.

Then by Lemma
A.4
, we have

\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))\leq\log|\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{2L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}}{\varepsilon}\Bigg)=\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8\exp(4B/\tau)L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg).

The proof is completed.
∎

The outer error can be bounded as below.

Lemma D.2
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|\lesssim\frac{B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}\log(1+{8\exp(4B/\tau)nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}+B\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}.

Proof.

Since
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
, we have
h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
.
Then by Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
D.1
,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S_{4}}(\mathcal{H}_{1})\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha>0}4\alpha+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{1/n}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{1},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{1/n}^{2B}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8\exp(4B/\tau)L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg)}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8\exp(4B/\tau)nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}(2B-{1}/{n})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8\exp(4B/\tau)nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}

Combined with Proposition
A.1
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{R}_{S_{4}}(\mathcal{H}_{1})+12B\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}

(35)

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}\log(1+{8\exp(4B/\tau)nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}+B\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(36)

Similar inequality holds for another direction. The proof is completed.
∎

D.2
Estimation of the inner error

For any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{Y}
,
we define the following function class and dataset:

\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneq\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\},\quad S^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}.

(37)

We bound the covering number
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S^{\prime}))
in the following lemma.

Lemma D.3
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, then for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},S^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots\,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}
, there holds

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S^{\prime}))\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg).

Proof.

We define
S_{3}=\{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}
.
Let
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\cdot),\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\cdot)
be two functions in
\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}
.
We choose a cover
\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}
of
\mathcal{W}
such that Eq. (
17
) holds. According to Eq. (
21
), for any
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
, we have

\displaystyle|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|\leq 4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in\{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}\leq 4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon.

It implies that

\displaystyle\|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\cdot)-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\cdot)\|_{L_{2}(S^{\prime})}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Big(4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in S_{3}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}\Big)^{2}}

\displaystyle=4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in S_{3}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}\leq 4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon.

Letting
\varepsilon=\mathbf{\epsilon}/(\leq 4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l})
yields

\displaystyle\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S^{\prime}))\leq\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{F},\mathbf{\epsilon}/(4\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}),L_{\infty,2}(S_{3}))\leq\log|C_{\varepsilon}|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg).

The proof is completed.
∎

The inner error can be estimated in the following lemma.

Lemma D.4
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}))\Bigg|=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)(B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\tau\sqrt{\log(n/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

Proof.

We define

\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneq\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\tau\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-2B,2B]\}.

Then we have
\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\in[-4B/\tau,4B/\tau]
and
\tau\exp(\cdot/\tau)
is
\exp(4B/\tau)
-Lipschitz over this interval. For any
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-2B,2B]
, there holds

0\leq\tau\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)\leq\tau\exp(4B/\tau).

Then
By
Eqs. (
13
), (
14
) and Proposition
A.1
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}|h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\tau\log\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg)-\tau\log\Bigg(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\Bigg(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Bigg[\!\!\min_{|\mu|\leq 2B}\!\!\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)\!-\!\tau\!+\!\mu\Bigg]-\Bigg[\!\min_{|\mu|\leq 2B}\!\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\frac{\Delta(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)-\tau+\mu\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},|\mu|\leq 2B}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\!\exp\left(\frac{\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)-\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp\left(\frac{\Delta(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})-\mu}{\tau}\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{I}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})+3\tau\exp(4B/\tau)\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}

with probability at least
1-\delta/2
. We define

\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneq\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mu,\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-2B,2B]\}.

According to Lemma
A.2
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}|h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\exp(4B/\tau)\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})+3\tau\exp(4B/\tau)\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}.

(38)

Note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})-\mu)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\mu.

We will control two terms respectively.

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mu\in[-2B,2B]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\mu\leq 2B\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\right|\leq 2B\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\right|^{2}}=\frac{2B}{\sqrt{m}}.

We apply Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
D.3
to obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha>0}4\alpha+\frac{12}{\sqrt{m}}\int_{\alpha}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S^{\prime}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

(39)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{m}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{m}}\int_{1/m}^{2B}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S^{\prime}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

(40)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{m}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{m}}\int_{1/m}^{2B}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg)}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{m}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{m}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}(2B-{1}/{m})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\frac{B}{\sqrt{m}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}.

(41)

Therefore,

\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{K}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})\lesssim\frac{B}{\sqrt{m}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}.

Plugging it into Eq. (
38
) yields

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\lesssim\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)(B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\tau\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}

(42)

with probability at least
1-\delta
.
By the independence of
S^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}
, with probability at least
1-\delta/n
, the following holds for all
i\in[n]
:

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\lesssim\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)(B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\tau\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}.

Taking summation yields

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}))\lesssim\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)(B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\tau\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}.

The other side of the inequality holds for the same reason. The proof of the lemma is completed.
∎

Now we prove Theorem
4.6
.

Proof of Theorem
4.6
.

Plugging Lemma
4.4
,
D.4
into the error decomposition Eq. (
16
), with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})|

\displaystyle\leq{\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|}+{\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\bigl)\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}\log(1+{8\exp(4B/\tau)nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}+B\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle+\frac{\exp(4B/\tau)(B\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}\log(1+{8mL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\tau\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}.

The proof is completed.
∎

D.3
Zero-shot Classification in SSCRL

In this subsection, we study the generalization performance of SSCRL in zero-shot classification task. We follow the framework in
42
. Given a scoring function
s
, the goal is to predict the label
c
of
\mathbf{x}
without training on a task-specific dataset.
Suppose there are
C
classes. For each class
c
, the distributions of data on two modalities
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
are denoted by
\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{X}}(c),\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(c)
respectively.
we study zero-shot classification performance in a probabilistic modeling regime. Specifically, for a label
c
, we model the Bayes rule
\mathbb{P}(c|\mathbf{x})
by

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}_{s}(c|\mathbf{x})=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim\mathcal{D}_{\mathcal{Y}}(c)}p_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbf{y})\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}}\exp(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})/\tau)}.

(43)

The performance of zero-shot classification is measured by KL-divergence between the Bayes rule and the modeled probability:

\mathcal{R}(s)=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}D_{\mathrm{KL}}(\mathbb{P}(c|\mathbf{x})||\mathbb{P}_{s}(c|\mathbf{x})).

Following
42
, we assume a conditional independence property of the data distribution.

Assumption D.5
.

For the joint distribution
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},c)
,
\mathbf{x}
and
c
are conditionally independent given
\mathbf{y}
. An special case is when
c
is a deterministic function of
\mathbf{y}
.

The following proposition shows that zero-shot classification error can be bounded by the pretraining excess risk.

Proposition D.6

(Proposition 2 in
42
)
.

Let Assumption
D.5
hold, then the error for zero-shot classification can be bounded by the excess risk of CRL.

\mathcal{R}(s)\leq\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s)-\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s).

We can adapt our generalization bound (Theorem
4.6
) and obtain

Theorem D.7
.

Let Assumptions
4.1
,
D.5
hold. Let
\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{1}\in\arg\min_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}(\mathbf{w})
.
Then with probability
1-\delta
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathcal{R}(s_{\hat{\mathbf{w}}_{1}})\!\leq\!\inf_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\!-\!\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s)\!+\!\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\!\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}\!\Bigg).

Appendix E
Extension to a general class of contrastive losses

In Lemma
4.2
, we formulate contrastive loss as minimization problems, which forms the theoretical foundation of our generalization analysis. In fact, it is closely related to the so-called
optimized certainty equivalent
(OCE). Another observation is that
CRL inherently relies on a
pairwise
structure, comparing positive and negative sample pairs via
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
.
In this section, we unify the OCE framework and general pairwise loss functions to formalize a broad family of CRL losses, where the canonical contrastive loss in Eq. (
1
) emerges as a natural special case. Building on this general formulation, we extend our earlier analyses of statistical consistency and generalization bounds to this larger class of losses—thereby enhancing the generality and applicability of our theoretical findings beyond the standard CRL setting.

Optimized certainty equivalent

We first introduce the OCE framework
(
9
)
, a powerful tool for risk-sensitive analysis.

Definition E.1

(OCE Risk)
.

Let
\phi:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}\cup\{+\infty\}
be a nondecreasing, closed, convex disutility function satisfying
\phi(0)=0
and
1\in\partial\phi(0)
. Suppose
\tau>0
is a hyperparameter. For a random variable
\mathbf{z}
and a measurable function
f
, the corresponding OCE risk is defined as

\oce^{\phi}(f)=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\Bigg\{\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\Bigg[\,\phi\Bigg(\frac{f(\mathbf{z})-\mu\,}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg]+\mu\Bigg\}.

This formulation encodes a risk-averse perspective by penalizing large realizations of the loss
f(\mathbf{z})

. Given
m
i.i.d. samples
\mathbf{z}_{1},\dots,\mathbf{z}_{m}
, the empirical OCE risk is

\oce_{m}^{\phi}(f)=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\bigg\{\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{f(\mathbf{z}_{j})-\mu\,}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\bigg\}.

The OCE framework subsumes a broad class of well-known risk measures as special cases, with illustrative examples provided in Table
2

(
34
)
. Notably, Lemma
4.2
establishes that the canonical contrastive loss corresponds to the OCE with the exponential disutility function
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1
, meaning standard contrastive loss is itself a specific case of the more general OCE risk.

Table 2
:
Examples of OCE risk
. For
\alpha\in(0,1)
,
\mathrm{VaR}_{\alpha}(f(\mathbf{z}))
denotes the
\alpha
-quantile of the distribution of
f(\mathbf{z})
,
[t]_{+}=\max\{0,t\}.

Name

\phi(t)

\oce^{\phi}(f)

Expected loss

t

\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{z})]

Entropy risk

\exp(t)-1

\log\mathbb{E}[\exp(f(\mathbf{z}))]

Mean-variance

\tfrac{1}{2}t^{2}+t(t\geq-1)

\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{z})]+\tfrac{1}{2}\operatorname{Var}(f(\mathbf{z}))

Conditional Value-at-Risk (CVaR)

\tfrac{1}{\alpha}[t]_{+}

\mathbb{E}[\,f(\mathbf{z})\mid f(\mathbf{z})\geq\mathrm{VaR}_{\alpha}(f(\mathbf{z}))\,]

A key advantage of framing CRL through the OCE lens lies in its ability to explain the hard negative mining property of contrastive loss
(
43
)
. In fact,
a key property of the OCE risk is its inherent risk aversion: the property
\phi(x)\geq x

implies
\oce^{\phi}(f)\geq\mathbb{E}[f(\mathbf{z})]
, making it a stronger criterion than the standard expected risk. This risk-averse nature compels CRL to prioritize hard negative examples, i.e., those with large
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}
that are more challenging to distinguish.

Pairwise loss

Next, we note the pairwise nature of contrastive loss function. The goal of CRL is to discriminate positive data from negative data, i.e. increasing the score difference
\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
between positive pairs and negative pairs. This aligns naturally with the pairwise learning paradigm, which employs pairwise losses for discriminative tasks
(
22
)
, motivating us to adopt a general pairwise loss formulation.
Specifically, we introduce a general pairwise loss:
\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))
, where
\ell:\mathbb{R}\to\mathbb{R}
is a nondecreasing function. Representative instantiations of
\ell
include
\ell(t)=\exp(t)
,
\ell(t)=\log(\exp(t)+1)
, and
\ell(t)=(\max(0,1+t))^{2}
.

By integrating the OCE framework with general pairwise loss, we propose the following contrastive loss for a pairwise loss
\ell
and a general disutility function
\phi
:

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg].

(44)

Notably, the contrastive loss Eq. (
1
) is a special case of our general contrastive loss framework when choosing
\ell(t)=t,\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1
.

Most existing work considers pairwise loss of the form

T(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\left[\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\right].

However, this standard expected risk has a critical limitation in practical CRL scenarios: the amount of negative examples is much larger than that of positive examples, and it fails to prioritize hard negative examples. However, the OCE-augmented pairwise loss
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
inherits the advantages of both components: it retains the pairwise nature of CRL loss while leveraging the risk-averse property of OCE to focus more on hard negative examples. Compared to the standard expected risk
T(s_{\mathbf{w}})
,
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
is more robust in imbalanced sample scenarios. Formally, we have the inequality
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\geq T(s_{\mathbf{w}})
, which implies that
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
is a stronger risk measure. This strength entails a one-way implication: minimizing
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
guarantees a small
T(s_{\mathbf{w}})
, but a small
T(s_{\mathbf{w}})
does not necessarily imply a small
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
.

In the rest of the paper, we assume
\phi
satisfies Definition
E.1
.

E.1
Problem formulations

Now we extend the settings in Section
2
to
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})
and define the statistical consistency.

E.1.1
Supervised setting

Similar to Lemma
4.2
,
the population risk for SCRL with
\phi,\ell
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{+}(\cdot|\mathbf{x})}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{-}(\cdot|\mathbf{x})}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg].

(45)

To estimate it, we draw
n
i.i.d. anchor points
\mathbf{x}_{i}\sim p_{\mathcal{X}}
for
i\in[n]
. For each anchor
\mathbf{x}_{i}
, we sample one positive example
\mathbf{y}_{i}\sim p_{+}(\cdot|\mathbf{x}_{i})
and then
m
negative examples
\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}\sim p_{-}(\cdot|\mathbf{x}_{i}):j\in[m]\}
.
The empirical risk is defined by

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}\Bigg].

(46)

An interesting property of OCE is its relationship with distributional robust optimization (DRO)
(
9
)
.
Let
\mathbf{p}=(p_{1},\cdots,p_{m})\in\Delta_{m}
, where
\Delta_{m}
is the simplex satisfying
\Delta_{m}=\{\mathbf{p}\in\mathbb{R}^{m}:\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}=1,p_{j}\geq 0\}
. Let
\varphi
be a proper closed
convex function and has a minimum value zero that is attained at
t=1
. The
\varphi
-divergence is defined as
D_{\varphi}(\mathbf{p},\mathbf{q})=\sum_{j=1}^{m}q_{j}\varphi(p_{j}/q_{j})
.
Then we have

Proposition E.2
.

Let
\varphi^{*}(s)=\max_{t\geq 0}ts-\varphi(t)
be the convex conjugate of
\varphi
. For
\phi=\varphi^{*}
, we have

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\max_{\mathbf{p}_{i}\in\Delta_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{ij}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p}_{i},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)\Bigg].

(47)

The DRO objective considers a distribution shift over an uncertainty set, thus helping the model to be more robust. The term
\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p}_{i},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)
controls the deviation from the uniform distribution
\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}
. When

\varphi(t)=t\log t-t+1
,
\varphi
-divergence corresponds to KL divergence. In this case, we can formulate Eq. (
6
) as DRO.

\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\max_{\mathbf{p}_{i}\in\Delta_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{ij}(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\tau D_{\mathrm{KL}}\left(\mathbf{p}_{i},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)\Bigg].

(48)

Proof of Proposition
E.2
.

We first prove that for any
z_{1},\cdots,z_{m}
, there holds

\max_{\mathbf{p}\in\triangle_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}z_{j}-\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mu+\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi^{*}((z_{j}-\mu)/\tau).

(49)

By Lagrangian duality theory, we have

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{p}\in\triangle_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}z_{j}-\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\max_{p_{j}\geq 0}\mu(1-\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j})+\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}z_{j}-\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\max_{p_{j}\geq 0}\mu(1-\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j})+\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{j}z_{j}-\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi(mp_{j})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\mu+\max_{p_{j}\geq 0}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}(mp_{j}(z_{j}-\mu)/\tau-\varphi(mp_{j}))\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\left\{\mu+\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\max_{p_{j}\geq 0}(mp_{j}(z_{j}-\mu)/\tau-\varphi(mp_{j}))\right\}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mu+\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi^{*}((z_{j}-\mu)/\tau),

where the last equality is by the definition of
\varphi^{*}
. Hence, Eq. (
49
) holds true. Back to Eq. (
47
), for
i\in[n]
, let
z_{j}=\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))
, applying Eq. (
49
) implies that

\displaystyle\max_{\mathbf{p}_{i}\in\Delta_{m}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}p_{ij}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\tau D_{\varphi}\left(\mathbf{p}_{i},\frac{\mathbf{1}}{m}\right)=\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\varphi^{*}\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}.

Taking summation over
i
completes the proof.
∎

The above proposition also helps to connect OCE with other fields, for instance, the general scoring rule analyzed in
46
.

E.1.2
Self-supervised setting

Given an anchor
\mathbf{x}
, we define the positive and negative conditional distributions as

p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})=p(\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x}),p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})=p_{\mathcal{Y}}(\mathbf{y}).

(50)

We define the population risk as

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}|\mathbf{x}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg].

(51)

To estimate it from data,
we draw
n
positive pairs
\{(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\}_{i=1}^{n}\sim p
. Additionally, we sample
m
negative examples
\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}\overset{\text{i.i.d.}}{\sim}p_{\mathcal{Y}}
. Each anchor
\mathbf{x}_{i}
is then contrasted against with all
\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime},j\in[m]
.
The empirical risk is defined to be

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\min_{\mu_{i}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}\Bigg].

(52)

E.1.3
Statistical consistency

We will also study the statistical consistency of
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}
. We denote
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*}=\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
, where the infimum is taken over all measurable scoring functions
s:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
. Recall that
\mathcal{E}^{*}=\sup_{s}\mathcal{E}(s)
over all measurable scoring functions
s:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
.

Definition E.3
.

The contrastive loss
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
is said to be statistically consistent with respect to
\mathcal{E}(s)
if, for any sequence of scoring functions
\{s_{n}\}
, the following holds over all joint distributions on
\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}
.

\text{If}\ \mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{n})\to\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*}\quad\text{then}\quad\mathcal{E}(s_{n})\to\mathcal{E}^{*}.

E.2
Useful lemmas about OCE

Now we introduce an interesting property of the empirical OCE w.r.t.
\ell_{\infty}
-norm.

Lemma E.4
.

For any
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},\cdots,u_{m}),\bar{\mathbf{u}}=(\bar{u}_{1},\cdots,\bar{u}_{m})
, we define

q(\mathbf{u})=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu,\quad q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\bar{u}_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu

then there holds

\left|q(\mathbf{u})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})\right|\leq\|\mathbf{u}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\infty}=\max_{j}|u_{j}-\bar{u}_{j}|.

As a special case, we take
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1
and derive that
\tau\log[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\exp(u_{j}/\tau)/m]
is
1
-
\ell_{\infty}
-Lipschitz. This property plays an important role in controlling the covering numbers.

Proof of Lemma
E.4
.

We assume that

\mu_{1}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{j}\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu,\mu_{2}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\bar{u}_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu.

By the optimality, we have

0\in-\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+1,0\in-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{\bar{u}_{j}-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+1.

(53)

Without loss of generality, we suppose
q(\mathbf{u})\geq q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})
, then there holds

\displaystyle|q(\mathbf{u})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})|

\displaystyle=q(\mathbf{u})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{1}-\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\bar{u}_{j}-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\mu_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Bigg(\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\phi\Bigg(\frac{\bar{u}_{j}-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg)+\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)(u_{j}-\mu_{1}-\bar{u}_{j}+\mu_{2})+\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)(u_{j}-\bar{u}_{j})+(\mu_{1}-\mu_{2})\Bigg(1-\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg),

where the inequality is due to the convexity of
\phi
. Since
\phi
is nondecreasing, we have
\partial\phi(t)\geq 0
. Combining Eq. (
53
) yields

\displaystyle|q(\mathbf{u})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})|

\displaystyle=q(\mathbf{u})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}})\leq\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)|u_{j}-\bar{u}_{j}|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\partial\phi\Bigg(\frac{u_{j}-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg)\max_{j}|u_{j}-\bar{u}_{j}|=\max_{j}|u_{j}-\bar{u}_{j}|=\|\mathbf{u}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}\|_{\infty}.

The proof is completed.
∎

The following lemma indicates that we can constrain the parameter
\mu
of OCE in a bounded domain.

Lemma E.5
.

Suppose the function
f
satisfies
f(\mathbf{z})\in[a,b]
for all
\mathbf{z}
. Then for any
\tau
, there holds

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((f(\mathbf{z})-\mu)/\tau)+\mu=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[a,b]}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((f(\mathbf{z})-\mu)/\tau)+\mu,

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi((f(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mu)/\tau)+\mu=

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[a,b]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi((f(\mathbf{z}_{i})-\mu)/\tau)+\mu.

Proof.

We define

\lambda(\mu)=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((f(\mathbf{z})-\mu)/\tau)+\mu.

To prove the first inequality, it suffices to prove that
\lambda(b+\epsilon)\geq\lambda(b),\lambda(a-\epsilon)\geq\lambda(a)
for any
\epsilon>0
.

To show that
\lambda(b+\epsilon)\geq\lambda(b)
for
\epsilon>0
. We define
X=f(\mathbf{z})-b\in[a-b,0]
. Then we have

\displaystyle\lambda(b+\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((f(\mathbf{z})-b-\epsilon)/\tau)+b+\epsilon=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((X-\epsilon)/\tau)+b+\epsilon

\displaystyle\geq\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}(\phi(X/\tau)-\epsilon/\tau)+b+\epsilon=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi(X/\tau)+b=\lambda(b),

where the inequality is because
\phi
is convex having
1
as a sub gradient at
0
.

To show that
\lambda(a-\epsilon)\geq\lambda(a)
for any
\epsilon>0
. We define
Y=f(\mathbf{z})-a\in[0,b-a]
. Then we have

\displaystyle\lambda(a-\epsilon)

\displaystyle=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((f(\mathbf{z})-a+\epsilon)/\tau)+a-\epsilon=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi((Y+\epsilon)/\tau)+a-\epsilon

\displaystyle\geq\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}(\phi(Y/\tau)+\epsilon/\tau)+a-\epsilon=\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{z}}\phi(Y/\tau)+a=\lambda(a),

where the inequality is because
\phi
is convex having
1
as a sub gradient at
0
.
Therefore, the first part in the lemma has been proved. The second part follows similar arguments.
∎

The following lemma shows that the expectation of empirical OCE risk can be upper bounded by the population OCE risk.

Lemma E.6
.

Let
\phi
be a disutility function,
\tau>0
. Let
z
be a random variable,
\{z_{1},\cdots,z_{m}\}
be
m
copies of
z
. Then there holds

\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{z}\phi((z-\mu)/\tau)+\mu\geq\mathbb{E}_{z_{j}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi((z_{j}-\mu)/\tau)+\mu.

Proof.

Let
\mu_{*}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{z}\phi((z-\mu)/\tau)+\mu
, then

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{z_{j}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi((z_{j}-\mu)/\tau)+\mu

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{z_{j}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi((z_{j}-\mu_{*})/\tau)+\mu_{*}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{z}{\tau}\phi((z-\mu_{*})/\tau)+\mu_{*}=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{z}\phi((z-\mu)/\tau)+\mu.

The proof is completed.
∎

E.3
Statistical consistency for
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)

In this subsection, we analyze the statistical consistency of a general class of contrastive losses
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
.
To simplify the discussion and avoid measure-theoretic complications, we assume that both
\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}
are discrete spaces throughout Section
E.3
.

Assumption E.7
.

We assume that
\phi^{\prime\prime}(t)>0
,
\ell
is convex, differentiable, non-decreasing and
\ell^{\prime}(0)>0
.

Remark E.8
.

Similar assumptions for
\ell
have been made in
22
. The condition for
\phi
is used to analyze the optimal
\mu
in OCE through implicit function theorem. The above assumption can be satisfied in many cases, for instance,
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1,\frac{1}{2}t^{2}+t;\ell(t)=t,\exp(t)
.

Theorem E.9
.

Let Assumption
E.7
hold. Then
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
is statistically consistent with
\mathcal{E}(s)
.

Remark E.10
.

By taking
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1,\ell(t)=t
, the above theorem shows that standard contrastive loss
\mathcal{L}(s)
is statistically consistent, aligning with the result in Theorem
3.1
. However, the proof there depends on the explicit formulation of optimal scoring functions. While the analysis here does not require such knowledge. Furthermore,
our theorem demonstrates that there exists a wide class of contrastive losses with different
\phi,\ell
that performs well in downstream tasks, except for log-sum-exp based risk.

Compared with the pairwise loss studied in
22
, the risk
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
admits a more complicated OCE structure, which contains a convex function
\phi
and a minimization problem. The proof of the previous work relies on the monotonicity of
