\ell^{\prime}(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))
w.r.t.
\mathbf{y}
for each
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
. However, this property no longer holds in OCE. To address this issue, we show that the expectation of compositional derivative is decreasing.

Lemma E.11
.

Let Assumption
E.7
hold.
\psi(t)=\tau\phi(t/\tau)
.
h
is an arbitrary function. We further define

\displaystyle\mu(t)

\displaystyle=\argmin_{\mu}\mu+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu),

\displaystyle U(t)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))\ell^{\prime}(h(\mathbf{y})-t).

Then
U(t)
is a decreasing function of
t
.

Proof.

We first show that the optimal
\mu
is unique for each
t
. Suppose

\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in\argmin_{\mu}\mu+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu),\quad\mu_{1}>\mu_{2}.

Then for any
\mathbf{y}
,
\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{1}<\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{2}
. Since
\psi
is a strictly convex function, we have

\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{1})-\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{2})<0.

By the optimality condition, we have
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{i})=1,i=1,2
. Then there holds

\displaystyle 0=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{1})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{2})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{1})-\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu_{2}))<0.

This contradiction implies that for each
t
, the optimal
\mu
is unique, which we denote by
\mu(t)
. Hence,

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))=1

(54)

for any
t
. For
t\geq s
, we prove that
U(t)\leq U(s)
.

\displaystyle(t-s)U(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))\ell^{\prime}(h(\mathbf{y})-t)(t-s)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))+\mu(s)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))+\mu(t)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))[(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))-(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu(s)-\mu(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))-\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t)))+\mu(s)-\mu(t),

where the second equality is due to Eq. (
54
), two inequalities result from the convexity of
\ell,\psi
. Applying similar methods, we have

\displaystyle(t-s)U(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))-\psi(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t)))+\mu(s)-\mu(t)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))[(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))-(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mu(s)-\mu(t)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))+\mu(s)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t)-\mu(t))+\mu(t)]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-t))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h(\mathbf{y})-s)-\mu(s))\ell^{\prime}(h(\mathbf{y})-s)(t-s)=U(s)(t-s).

Since
t\geq s
, we have
U(t)\leq U(s)
.
The proof is completed.
∎

For a function
h:\mathcal{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
, an anchor point
\mathbf{x}
, disutility function
\phi
and pairwise loss
\ell
,
we define

\displaystyle Q_{\mathbf{x}}^{\phi,\ell}(h)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(h(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h(\mathbf{y}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg].

(55)

Then
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}Q_{\mathbf{x}}^{\phi,\ell}(s(\mathbf{x},\cdot))
. Therefore,
s_{*}
is the minimum of
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
if and only if
s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\cdot)
is the minimum of
Q^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathbf{x}}
for all
\mathbf{x}
.
Let
\mathcal{B}
be the set of all scoring functions that maximize
\mathcal{E}(s)
. According to Lemma
3.3
, we have

\mathcal{B}=\Bigg\{s:\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}>\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))>0,\quad\text{if}\,\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}\neq\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg\}.

To prove Theorem
E.9
, we present the following lemma.

Lemma E.12
.

Let Assumption
E.7
hold, then we have

\inf_{s\notin\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)>\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s).

Proof of Lemma
E.12
.

We prove by contradiction. Note that
\inf_{s\notin\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)\geq\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
.
If the lemma does not hold true, we have
\inf_{s\notin\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)=\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
. Then there exists an optimal scoring function
s_{*}
such that
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{*})=\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*},s_{*}\notin\mathcal{B}
. Therefore, there exists
\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2}\in\mathcal{Y}
with

\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}_{1}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}>0,s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}_{1})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}_{2})\leq 0.

According to Eq. (
55
),
h_{*}(\cdot)\coloneq s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\cdot)
is a minimum of
Q^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathbf{x}}
.
We introduce a function
h_{1}
s.t.
h_{1}(\mathbf{y})\neq 0
if
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{y}_{1}
and
h_{1}(\mathbf{y}_{1})=1
. Let
g(\beta)=Q^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathbf{x}_{1}}(h_{*}+\beta h_{1})
for
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, then
g
is convex. By the optimality of
h_{*}
, we have
g^{\prime}(0)=0
.

For simplicity, we denote
\psi={\tau}\phi(\cdot/\tau)
, then
\psi^{\prime\prime}>0
and

g(\beta)=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y})+\beta(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y})))-\mu)+\mu.

For
\beta
and
\mathbf{y}
, we define the optimal
\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))
as

\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))=\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y})+\beta(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y})))-\mu)+\mu.

It satisfies the following equality

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y})+\beta(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y})))-\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y})))-1=0.

(56)

When
\beta=0
, there holds
\mu(0,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))=\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))
by the definition of
\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))
in Lemma
E.11
.
Since
\psi^{\prime\prime}>0
, implicit function theorem guarantees the existence of
\frac{\partial\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))}{\partial\beta}
. Therefore,

\displaystyle g^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(0,h_{*}(\mathbf{y})))\left(\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))}{\partial\beta}\Bigg|_{\beta=0}\right)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))}{\partial\beta}\Bigg|_{\beta=0}\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mu(\beta,h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))}{\partial\beta}\Bigg|_{\beta=0}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(0,h_{*}(\mathbf{y})))-1)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))(h_{1}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-h_{1}(\mathbf{y})),

where the last equality is due to Eq. (
56
) with
\beta=0
.
By noting
g^{\prime}(0)=0
and the definition of
h_{1}
, we further have

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}/\mathbf{y}_{1}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))d\mathbf{y}=0,

(57)

In a similar way, we get

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}/\mathbf{y}_{2}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))d\mathbf{y}=0.

(58)

Combining Eqs. (
57
), (
58
) yields

A+B+C+D=0,

(59)

where we define

\displaystyle A=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}/\{\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2}\}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})\Bigg[\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))\Bigg]d\mathbf{y},

\displaystyle B=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathcal{Y}}p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})[(\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})))]d\mathbf{y},

\displaystyle C=

\displaystyle p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})\psi^{\prime}(\ell(0)-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(0)

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))),

\displaystyle D=

\displaystyle(p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})\psi^{\prime}(\ell(0)-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))\ell^{\prime}(0)).

Now we bound each term respectively. Since
\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})}<\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})},h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})\leq h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})
, by the convexity of
\psi,\ell
, we have

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle\leq\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y})),

\displaystyle\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle\leq\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y})),\quad\forall\mathbf{y}\in\mathcal{Y}/\{\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{2}\}.

Therefore,
A\leq 0
.

Applying Lemma
E.11
yields

\displaystyle B=U(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2}))-U(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\leq 0.

Now we control
C
and
D
. Note that
0<\ell^{\prime}(0)\leq\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})),\ell(0)\leq\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))
. Hence, there holds

\displaystyle\psi^{\prime}(\ell(0)-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(0)\leq\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1})).

As a result,

C\leq p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{1})\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}_{2})}\psi^{\prime}(\ell(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))-\mu(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))\ell^{\prime}(h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{2})-h_{*}(\mathbf{y}_{1}))<0.

In a similar manner, we can prove that
D<0
.

Combining above results together, we have

A+B+C+D<0,

which is contrary to Eq. (
59
). Therefore, the lemma holds true.
∎

Proof of Theorem
E.9
.

By Lemma
E.12
, we set
\delta=\inf_{s\notin\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)-\inf_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)>0
. Suppose
\{s_{n}\}
be a sequence such that
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{n})\to\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*}
, then there exists
N>0
such that

\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{n}))-\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*}<\delta/2<\inf_{s\notin\mathcal{B}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)-\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{*},\quad n\geq N.

Therefore, for
n\geq N
, we have
h_{n}\in\mathcal{B}
. It means that
R(h_{n})=R^{*}
. Hence,
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
is statistically consistent with
\mathcal{E}(s)
.
∎

Now we derive the calibration-style inequality, relating the excess risks of CRL and downstream task.
We make the following assumptions.

Assumption E.13
.

We assume the pairwise loss
\ell
is linear:
\ell(t)=t
.
Furthermore, suppose that
|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq B
and
\phi^{\prime\prime}(t)\in[\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}]
over
[-2B,2B]
, where
\gamma_{2}>\gamma_{1}>0
. For the optimal scoring function
s_{*}\in\argmin_{s}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)
, we assume that
|s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq B
. Additionally,
for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
, there exists a convex function
\kappa
with
\kappa(0)=0
such that

(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))^{2}\geq\kappa\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg).

Theorem E.14
.

Let Assumption
E.13
hold. Then we have

\mathcal{L}(s)-\min_{s}\mathcal{L}(s)\gtrsim\kappa(\max_{s}\mathcal{E}(s)-\mathcal{E}(s)).

Remark E.15
.

For log-sum-exp contrastive loss
\mathcal{L}(s)
, the optimal scoring functions are captured in Lemma
3.2
. According to Eq. (
32
), Assumption
E.13
holds
\kappa(t)=4\tau^{2}t^{2}/\alpha^{2}
. We could get similar results in Theorem
3.4
.

Proof of Theorem
E.14
.

We denote
\psi(\cdot)=\tau\phi(\cdot/\tau)
for simplicity.
We define the norm
\|\cdot\|
by
\|s\|=\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})^{2}}
. Let

\displaystyle g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\frac{s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\|s-s_{*}\|}.

(60)

We define
s_{r}=s_{*}+rg_{1},r\in[0,\|h-h_{*}\|]
, then
s_{0}=s_{*},s_{\|h-h_{*}\|}=s,\sup_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}|s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq B
. We further define

G(r)=\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{r})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu)+\mu.

We define the optimal
\mu
for each triple
(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
as

\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu)+\mu.

By Lemma
E.5
and
s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
, we can suppose that
\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\in[-2B,2B]
.
Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))=1.

(61)

Therefore,

\displaystyle G^{\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}]+\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-1)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})).

By the optimality of
s_{*}
,
G
has a minimum
0
, implying that
G^{\prime}(0)=0
. We further have

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\!-\!s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\!-\!\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\!-\!g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))\!-\!\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}](g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\!-\!g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})).

Taking derivative to Eq. (
61
) w.r.t.
r
, we know that

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r})=0.

(62)

We define the density function for each
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},r)
:

q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}.

Then by Eq. (
62
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}

\displaystyle=\int q_{{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}}(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})).

Consequently,

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime}(r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}](g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}](g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r})\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}]^{2},

(63)

where the first equality is due to Eq. (
62
).
By the definition of
q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})))

\displaystyle\times

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\frac{\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))}[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})))\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\frac{\partial\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})}{\partial r}]^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\psi^{\prime\prime}(s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{r}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\mu(r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}(g(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))]^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}}{\tau}\mathrm{Var}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))],

(64)

where the last inequality results from
\psi^{\prime\prime}(t)=\phi^{\prime\prime}(t/\tau)/\tau\geq\gamma_{1}/\tau
. According to Lemma
3.2
, we can choose
s_{*}
such that for all
\mathbf{x}
,
\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
. From the definition of
q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}
, we know that
q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})/p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\geq\gamma_{1}/\gamma_{2}
for all
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
. Hence, for fixed
r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
,

\displaystyle\mathrm{Var}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}[(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})]=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}\sim q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}}(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}))^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int\int q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})q_{r,\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}))^{2}d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}d\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{2\gamma_{2}^{2}}\int\int p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}))^{2}d\mathbf{y}^{\prime}d\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{2\gamma_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{2\gamma_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})^{2}+g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})^{2})-\frac{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime\prime})=\frac{\gamma_{1}^{2}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})^{2},

(65)

where the first inequality is due to
\mathrm{Var}[X]=\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{X,X^{\prime}}(X-X^{\prime})^{2}
, the last inequality is by

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\frac{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\|s-s_{*}\|_{2}}=0.

Combining Eqs. (
63
), (
64
), (
65
) together, we have

\displaystyle G^{\prime\prime}(r)\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})^{2}=\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}g_{1}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\frac{(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))^{2}}{\|s-s_{*}\|^{2}_{2}}=\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}.

Applying Taylor’s expansion yields

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)-\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{*})=

\displaystyle G(\|s-s_{*}\|)-G(0)=G^{\prime}(0)\|s-s_{*}\|+\frac{1}{2}G^{\prime\prime}(\xi)\|s-s_{*}\|^{2},\quad\xi\in[0,\|s-s_{*}\|]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{2\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))^{2}.

Then we make the following decomposition that introduces the pair
\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|^{2}+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|^{2}+|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}).

(66)

We will show that for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}
,

\displaystyle|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|^{2}+|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{I}\Bigg[\Bigg(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg)(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0\Bigg]\kappa\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg).

(67)

It is trivial when RHS is equal to
0
. We consider the case

(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0,\quad\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\neq 0.

Without loss of generality, suppose that

\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}>\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})},\quad s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq 0.

Therefore,

\displaystyle|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|^{2}+|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})+s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}|s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})+s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})+s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))^{2}\geq\frac{1}{2}\kappa\Bigg(\frac{1}{2}\Bigg|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}\Bigg|\Bigg).

As a result, Eq. (
E.3
) holds true.

According to Eq. (
26
),

\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)\leq\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0]|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}|.

Therefore,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s)-\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{*})\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{2\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}\sim p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s_{*}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}))^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{8\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\Bigg[\mathbb{I}[(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0]\kappa(\frac{1}{2}|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}|)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{8\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\Bigg[\kappa(\frac{1}{2}|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}|\mathbb{I}[(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0])\Bigg]

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{8\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\kappa(\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\sim\mathbf{p}_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}}\mathbb{I}[(\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})})(s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-s(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\leq 0]|\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y})}-\frac{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{+}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{p_{\mathbf{x}}^{-}(\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}|)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{\gamma_{1}^{3}}{8\gamma_{2}^{2}\tau}\kappa(\mathcal{E}^{*}-\mathcal{E}(s)),

where
the first equality results from
\kappa(0)=0
,
the last inequality is due to Jenson’s inequality and the convexity of
\kappa
.
The proof is completed.
∎

E.4
Generalization analysis of SCRL for general OCE and pairwise loss

In this subsection, we give generalization analysis of SCRL for general OCE and pairwise loss. For disutility function
\phi
and pairwise loss
\ell
, recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg],

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{S}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg[\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}\Bigg].

We aim to control the uniform convergence bound

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|.

We make similar error decomposition as Eq. (
12
).

\displaystyle\Big|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|\leq\underbrace{\Big|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]\Big|}_{\text{inner error}}+\underbrace{\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|}_{\text{outer error}}.

(68)

We will estimate inner error and outer error respectively.

To start,
we impose mild assumptions on the pairwise loss function
\ell
and the disutility function
\phi
.

Assumption E.16
.

Assume that
\ell
is
G
-Lipschitz continuous over the interval
[-2B,2B]
. Moreover, there exists a constant
M>0
such that
\ell(t)\in[-M,M]
for all
t\in[-2B,2B]
.

Assumption E.17
.

The disutility function
\phi
is
\eta
-Lipschitz continuous over the interval
[-{2M}/{\tau},{2M}/{\tau}]
.

Assumption E.18
.

Suppose that
\phi
is
\gamma
-strongly convex over the interval
[-{2M}/{\tau},2M/{\tau}]
, where
\gamma>0
denotes the strong convexity parameter.

Remark E.19
.

It is worth noting that the above assumptions do not require
\ell
or
\phi
to satisfy Lipschitz continuity or strong convexity globally over
\mathbb{R}
—a condition that is often too restrictive for practical functions in CRL. Instead, our analysis is confined to the bounded domain of

\ell(t)
, which lies within
[-2B,2B]
when Assumption
4.1
holds. This boundedness renders Assumption
E.16
valid for most commonly used pairwise loss functions in practice.

By the same token, the Lipschitz continuity and strong convexity of
\phi
only need to hold over the bounded interval
[-2M/\tau,2M/\tau]
, which is induced by the boundedness of
\ell(t)
(Assumption
E.16
). For instance, consider the exponential disutility function
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1
, which is neither Lipschitz continuous nor strongly convex over the entire real line. However, over the bounded interval
[-2M/\tau,2M/\tau]
,
\phi
is
\exp(2M/\tau)
-Lipschitz continuous and
\exp(-2M/\tau)
-strongly convex.

E.4.1
Outer Error

For
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}
, we introduce
g_{\mathbf{w}}:\mathcal{X}\times\mathcal{Y}^{m+1}\to\mathbb{R}
as follows:

g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu.

Then we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})\Big]-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}\Bigg[\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}\Bigg)\Bigg]

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\Bigg(\min_{\mu_{i}\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{i}\Bigg)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}\Big[g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})\Big]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im}).

By Proposition
A.1
, we can estimate it through Rademacher complexity
\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{G})
, where

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}

\displaystyle=\left\{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})\mapsto g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}):\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\right\}.

and recall that

S=\{(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{1},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{11},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1m}),\cdots,(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{n1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{nm})\}.

According to Proposition
A.3
,

\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{G})
can be controlled by
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))
.
Let
S_{1},\mathcal{H}
be defined in Eqs. (
19
), (
20
).
Then we have

Lemma E.20
.

Let Assumptions
4.1
,
E.16
hold. Then we have

\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))\leq\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\epsilon}/(G),L_{\infty}(S_{1})).

Proof.

Let
g_{\mathbf{w}},g_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}
be two functions in
\mathcal{G}
. For
i\in[n]
, we define

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{i}=((\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1})),\cdots,\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im}))),\mathbf{u}_{i}^{\prime}=(\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1})),\cdots,\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im}))).

According to Lemma
E.4
, there holds

\displaystyle\|g_{\mathbf{w}}-g_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{2}(S)}=

\displaystyle\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})-g_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime}))^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{i}|g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})-g_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i}|q(\mathbf{u}_{i})-q(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i})|\leq\max_{i}\|\mathbf{u}_{i}-\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{i}\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\max_{i}\max_{j}|\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))-\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle G\max_{i}\max_{j}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{ij})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle G\max_{(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})\in S_{1}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\hat{\mathbf{x}},\hat{\mathbf{y}},\hat{\mathbf{y}}^{\prime})|=G\|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{\infty}(S_{1}))}.

Therefore,

\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))\leq\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\epsilon}/(G),L_{\infty}(S_{1})).

The proof is completed.
∎

The outer error for general
\phi,\ell
is stated as follows.

Lemma E.21
.

Suppose that Assumptions
4.1
,
E.16
,
E.17
hold. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{(GB+M)(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}})}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

Proof.

We first give a uniform bound for
g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime})
for
\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}
.
Let

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}=(\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1})),\cdots,\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1}))),\mathbf{u}^{\prime}=(\ell(0),\cdots,\ell(0))\in\mathbb{R}^{m}.

Let function
q(\cdot)
be defined in Lemma
E.4
. We have

q(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\tau\phi((\ell(0)-\mu)/\tau)+\mu\geq\ell(0)-\mu+\mu=\ell(0)

and the minimum can be obtained at
\mu=\ell(0)
. Therefore,
q(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})=\ell(0)
. Applying Lemma
E.4
to
\mathbf{u},\mathbf{u}^{\prime}

yields

\displaystyle|g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime})|=|q(\mathbf{u})|\leq

\displaystyle|q(\mathbf{u})-q(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|+|q(\mathbf{u}^{\prime})|\leq\|\mathbf{u}-\mathbf{u}^{\prime}\|_{\infty}+|\ell(0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}|\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))|+|\ell(0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}|\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\ell(0)|+2|\ell(0)|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle G\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathcal{X},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Y}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|+2|\ell(0)|\leq 2GB+2M,

where we have used the
G
-Lipschitzness of
\ell
in the last inequality.
Then

B_{\mathcal{G}}\coloneq\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\|g_{\mathbf{w}}\|_{L_{2}(S)}\leq\max_{i}\sup_{\mathbf{w}}|g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{im}^{\prime})|\leq 2GB+2M.

According to Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
A.5
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{G})\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha>0}4\alpha+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha}^{B_{\mathcal{G}}}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2GB+2M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{G},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2GB+2M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H},\mathbf{\epsilon}/G,L_{\infty}(S_{1}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\frac{1}{n}}^{2GB+2M}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg)}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8nL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}(2GB+2M-{1}/{n})=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{(GB+M)\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}}{n}\Bigg).

Using Proposition
A.1
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big[\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}}[g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m})]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}g_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{i1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{im})\Big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathfrak{R}_{S}(\mathcal{G})+12(GB+M)\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2n}}=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{(GB+M)(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}})}{\sqrt{n}}\right).

The same upper bound also holds for
the other side.
The proof is completed.
∎

E.4.2
Inner Error

In this part, we will estimate the inner error
|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})]|
. We apply algorithmic stability methods to handle with strongly convex
\phi
, and uniform convergence theory to study general
\phi
.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

(69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg\{\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg]\Bigg\}

According to Lemma
E.6
, for any
\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
, there holds

\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg]\geq 0.

Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\geq 0.

(70)

General
\phi

The inner error is similar to
generalization error of risk-averse learning in
34
. However, they only considered one random variable, while we need to deal with triplet
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
and a pairwise loss
\ell
, which requires a more refined analysis.

For
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
, according to Eq. (
37
), we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneq\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\},\quad S^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\}.

The inner error for general disutility function
\phi
is stated as follows.

Lemma E.22
.

Let Assumptions
4.1
,
E.16
,
E.17
hold. Then there holds

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\Big[\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big]\Big|=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M)}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

Proof.

For
\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
, we define

\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}})\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu.

(71)

By Lemma
E.5
and
\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}))\in[-M,M]
, we can assume that
\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}})\in[-M,M]
.
We further have

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}})}{\tau}\right)+\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}}).

(72)

Taking expectation over
{S^{\prime}}
yields

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\leq\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}})}{\tau}\right)+\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}({S^{\prime}}).

Let

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\coloneq\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\mapsto\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu:\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-M,M]\}.

Then we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu-\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\}}\left[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\right]

(73)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)+\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\left[\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)+\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)-\left[\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})}{\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-{\mu}}{\tau}\right)-\left[\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-{\mu}}{\tau}\right)\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}),

where the second inequality results from the convexity of
\sup
and Jenson’s inequality, the last inequality is due to Proposition
A.1
,
A.2
.
Now we derive an upper bound of
\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}(\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\mu.

(74)

We will control two terms respectively.

\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\mu\leq M\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\right|\leq M\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\right|^{2}}=\frac{M}{\sqrt{m}}.

(75)

Note that
\ell
is
G
-Lipschitz.
We apply Lemma
A.2
, Eq. (
39
) and derive

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))\leq G\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j})\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

(76)

Plugging Eqs. (
75
), (
76
) into Eq. (
74
) yields

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))+\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\sup_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbf{\epsilon}_{j}\mu\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

(77)

Combining Eq. (
73
) and Eq. (
77
), we know that for all
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu-\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\}}\left[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M)}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

(78)

By the convexity of
\sup
and Jenson’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg\{\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\right]-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\Bigg]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Bigg\{\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu-\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}\}}\left[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu\right]\Bigg\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M)}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg),

where the last inequality is due to Eq. (
78
).
The proof is completed by noting Eq. (
70
).
∎

Strongly convex
\phi

The OCE risk contains an inner minimization problem. For strongly convex
\phi
, we could obtain a fine-grained analysis through algorithmic stability, similar to Lemma
4.3
.

Lemma E.23
.

Let Assumptions
4.1
,
E.16
,
E.17
,
E.18
hold. Then we have

\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})-\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})\leq\frac{2\eta\tau}{\gamma m}.

Proof.

We denote
\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})=\tau\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu,\mu\in[-M,M]
. Then
\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
is
\gamma/\tau
-strongly convex w.r.t.
\mu
. We also have

\displaystyle\frac{\partial\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})}{\partial\mu}=1-\partial\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)\in[1-\eta,1].

By the property of
\phi
in Definition
E.1
, we have
\eta\geq 1
.
Therefore, for any
\mu_{1},\mu_{2}\in[-M,M]
,

|\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu_{1};\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu_{2};\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|\leq\eta|\mu_{1}-\mu_{2}|.

Let
\hat{\mu}(S^{\prime})
be defined in Eq. (
71
).
Then for any
S^{\prime}
,

\min_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\hat{\mu}(\{S^{\prime}\});\mathbf{y}^{\prime}),

therefore,

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\leq\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime}).

(79)

By the
\gamma/\tau
-strong convexity of
\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})
,
we can define a convex function

\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\coloneq\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{\gamma}{2\tau}\mu^{2}.

Then

\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime})=\argmin_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\hat{\Psi}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})+\frac{\gamma}{2\tau}\mu^{2},

which is the regularized empirical risk minimization algorithm.
According to Theorem 12, 22 in
12
, we have

\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]\leq\frac{2\eta\tau}{\gamma m}

holds uniformly for
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{w}
. Combined with Eq. (
79
), for any
\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{w}
, there holds

\displaystyle\min_{\mu\in[M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\mathbb{E}_{S^{\prime}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\bar{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}\}}\Bigg[\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\hat{\mu}_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(S^{\prime});\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\Bigg]\leq\frac{2\eta\tau}{\gamma m}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\!\Big[\!\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}(\mathbf{w})\!-\!\mathbb{E}\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})\!\Big]\!=\!\sup_{\mathbf{w}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\!\Bigg[\!\min_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}^{\prime})\!-\!\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime}}\min_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\Psi_{\mathbf{w},\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}(\mu;\mathbf{y}_{j}^{\prime})\!\Bigg]\!\leq\!\frac{2\eta\tau}{\gamma m}.

The proof is completed.
∎

Combining Lemma
E.21
,
E.22
,
E.23
with Eq. (
68
) establishes the generalization error for
\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(\mathbf{w})
.

Theorem E.24
.

Let Assumptions
4.1
,
E.16
,
E.17
hold, with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|=

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\eta(GB+M)(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}})(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}})\!\Bigg).

If Assumption
E.18
holds, we obtain a tighter bound

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathcal{L}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\hat{\mathcal{L}}^{\phi,\ell}_{\mathrm{S}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})=\widetilde{O}\left(\frac{(GB+M)(\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}+\sum_{l=1}^{L}\sqrt{d_{l}d_{l-1}})}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{2\eta\tau}{\gamma m}\right).

The above theorem indicates that strongly convex disutility function
\phi
admits a better generalization performance. A special case is
\phi(t)=\exp(t)-1
, corresponding to standard contrastive loss
\mathcal{L}
. This shows the advantage of the log-sum-exp structure.

E.5
Generalization analysis of SSCRL for general OCE and pairwise loss

In this subsection, we turn to the generalization analysis of SSCRL for general OCE and pairwise loss
Recall that

\displaystyle L_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}\Bigg[\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\Bigg],

\displaystyle\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\Bigg[\sum_{i=1}^{n}\min_{\mu_{i}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu_{i}}{\tau}\right)+\mu_{i}\Bigg].

We aim to bound the uniform convergence

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|.

Similar to the analysis in Section
D
, we make the following error decomposition:

\displaystyle|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})|

(80)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|}_{\text{outer error}}+\underbrace{\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\bigl)\Bigg|}_{\text{inner error}},

where we define

\displaystyle H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu,

\displaystyle\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\frac{\tau}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\phi\left(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{j}))-\mu}{\tau}\right)+\mu.

We will analyze inner error and outer error respectively.

E.5.1
Estimation of the outer error

Recall that
S_{4}=\{(\mathbf{x}_{1},\mathbf{y}_{1}),\cdots,(\mathbf{x}_{n},\mathbf{y}_{n})\}
,
we define the function class

\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}}=\{(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\mapsto H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\}.

We establish the following bound for the integral of the covering number
\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))
.

Lemma E.25
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, we have

\displaystyle\int_{1/n}^{\eta M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\eta M\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}\Bigg).

Proof.

we define

\mu_{1}\in\argmin_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu,\mu_{2}\in\argmin_{\mu\in[-M,M]}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu.

We suppose that
H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-H_{{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle|H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-H_{{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|=H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-H_{{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{1}}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu_{1}-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\mu_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\Bigg|\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)-\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu_{2}}{\tau}\Bigg)\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\eta|\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\eta G|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|,

where the first inequality is by the definition of
\mu_{1}
, the second and third inequalities use the Lipschitzness of
\phi,\ell
, respectively.

We choose a cover
\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}
of
\mathcal{W}
such that Eq. (
17
) holds. By Eq.
21
, we know that

\displaystyle|H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-H_{{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq\eta G\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}|\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})-\Delta_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime})|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\eta G\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in\{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4\eta G\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\max_{\mathbf{v}\in\mathcal{X},\mathcal{Y}}\|f_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{v})-f_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}(\mathbf{v})\|_{2}\leq 4\eta G\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon.

As a result,

\|h_{\mathbf{w}}-h_{\mathbf{w}^{\prime}}\|_{L_{2}(S_{4})}\leq 4\eta G\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}\varepsilon

For
\mathbf{\epsilon}>0
, choosing

\varepsilon=\frac{\mathbf{\epsilon}}{4\eta G\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}}.

Then we have

\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))\leq\log|\mathcal{C}_{\varepsilon}|\leq\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{2L\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s_{l}}{\varepsilon}\Bigg)=\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8\eta GL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg).

It implies that

\displaystyle\int_{1/n}^{\eta M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq

\displaystyle\int_{1/n}^{\eta M}\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log\Bigg(1+\frac{8\eta GL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}}{\mathbf{\epsilon}}\Bigg)}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}{d_{l}d_{l-1}}\log(1+{8\eta GnL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})}(\eta M-{1}/{n})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta M\log(1+{8\eta GnL\Pi_{l=1}^{L}s^{2}_{l}})\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}.

The proof is completed.
∎

The outer error can be estimated as follows

Lemma E.26
.

Let Assumption
4.1
, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta M(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigg)

Proof.

Since
\phi(0)=0,\phi(x)\geq x
, we have

\displaystyle H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})

\displaystyle=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-0}{\tau}\Bigg)+0

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\Bigg|\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))}{\tau}\Bigg)-0\Bigg|\leq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\eta|\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))|\leq\eta M.

and

H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})=\min_{\mu\in\mathbb{R}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\tau\phi\Bigg(\frac{\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu}{\tau}\Bigg)+\mu\geq\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}}\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))\geq-M.

By noting that
\eta\geq 1
, we have
|H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq\eta M
. For the similar reason, there also holds
\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})|\leq\eta M

Then by Proposition
A.3
and Lemma
E.25
, we have

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{S_{4}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}})\leq

\displaystyle\inf_{\alpha>0}4\alpha+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{\alpha}^{\eta M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}\leq\frac{4}{n}+\frac{12}{\sqrt{n}}\int_{1/n}^{\eta M}\sqrt{\log\mathcal{N}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}},\mathbf{\epsilon},L_{2}(S_{4}))}d\mathbf{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta M\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}}{\sqrt{n}}\Bigg).

Combined with Proposition
A.1
, we have with probability at least
1-\delta
,

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})

\displaystyle\leq 2\mathfrak{R}_{S_{4}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{w}})+6\eta M\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{n}}

(81)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta M(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)}}{\sqrt{n})}\Bigg).

(82)

Similar inequality holds for another direction.
The proof is completed.
∎

E.5.2
Estimation of the inner error

Lemma E.27
.

Let Assumption
4.1
hold, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, we have

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}))\Bigg|=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

Proof.

Recall that

\displaystyle\mathcal{G}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}=\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}),\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}\},\quad S^{\prime}=\{\mathbf{y}_{1}^{\prime},\cdots,\mathbf{y}_{m}^{\prime}\},

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}=\{\mathbf{y}^{\prime}\mapsto\ell(\Delta_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y},\mathbf{y}^{\prime}))-\mu:\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W},\mu\in[-M,M]\}.

By Proposition
A.1
and Eqs. (
73
), (
77
), for any fixed
(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})
, with probability at least
1-\delta
over the sampling of
\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{1},\cdots,\mathbf{y}^{\prime}_{m}
, there holds

\displaystyle H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta\mathfrak{R}_{S^{\prime}}(\mathcal{H}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}})+12\eta M\sqrt{\frac{\log(1/\delta)}{m}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M\sqrt{\log(2/\delta)})}{\sqrt{2m}}\Bigg)

By the independence of
S^{\prime}
and
\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}
, with probability at least
1-\delta/n
, the following holds for all
i\in[n]
:

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

Taking summation yields

\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}(H_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\widehat{H}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i}))=\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M\sqrt{\log(n/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg).

The other side of the inequality holds for the same reason. The proof of the lemma is completed.

∎

The generalization bound is stated in the following theorem.

Theorem E.28
.

Suppose Assumption
4.1
,
E.16
,
E.17
hold. Then with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}\Big|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}^{\phi,\ell}(s_{\mathbf{w}})\Big|\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\eta(GB+M)(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\sqrt{\log(n/\delta)})(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})\Bigg)

Proof.

Plugging Lemma
E.26
,
E.27
into the error decomposition Eq. (
80
), with probability at least
1-\delta
, there holds

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{w}\in\mathcal{W}}|\mathcal{L}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})-\widehat{\mathcal{L}}_{\mathrm{SS}}(s_{\mathbf{w}})|

\displaystyle\leq{\Bigg|\mathbb{E}_{\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y}}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x},\mathbf{y})-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\Bigg|}+{\Bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\bigl(h_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})-\hat{h}_{\mathbf{w}}(\mathbf{x}_{i},\mathbf{y}_{i})\bigl)\Bigg|}

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\frac{\eta M(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\sqrt{\log(1/\delta)})}{\sqrt{n}}+\frac{\eta(GB\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+M\sqrt{\log(n/\delta)})}{\sqrt{m}}\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{O}\Bigg(\eta(GB+M)(\sqrt{\sum_{l=1}^{L}d_{l}d_{l-1}}+\sqrt{\log(n/\delta)})(\frac{1}{\sqrt{m}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}})\Bigg).

The proof is completed.
∎

32
